# India at the 2026 Asian Games

[India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India "India") competed at the [2026 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Asian_Games "2026 Asian Games") in [Aichi Prefecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aichi_Prefecture "Aichi Prefecture"), Japan from 19 September to 4 October 2026. This was the country's [20th consecutive appearance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_at_the_Asian_Games "India at the Asian Games") in the competition, having made its [debut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_at_the_1951_Asian_Games "India at the 1951 Asian Games") at the inaugural [1951 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1951_Asian_Games "1951 Asian Games"). The Indian contingent consisted of 495 athletes competing in 36 sports. [Sports shooter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_shooting "Sports shooting") [Manu Bhaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manu_Bhaker "Manu Bhaker") and [kabaddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi "Kabaddi") player [Pawan Sehrawat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pawan_Sehrawat "Pawan Sehrawat") were the nation's flag-bearers during the opening ceremony. Shooter [Neeru Dhanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neeru_Dhanda "Neeru Dhanda") was the flag-bearer for the closing ceremony. India finished fourth in the [medal table](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Asian_Games_medal_table "2026 Asian Games medal table") with 85 medals including 21 gold, 27 silver, and 37 bronze medals. It was India's second highest medal tally at the Asian Games after the [2022 edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_at_the_2022_Asian_Games "India at the 2022 Asian Games").

## Background

The [Indian Olympic Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Olympic_Association "Indian Olympic Association") (IOA) was established in 1927 and recognised by the [International Olympic Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Olympic_Committee "International Olympic Committee") in the same year. India was one of the founding members of the [Asian Games Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Games_Federation "Asian Games Federation") (AGF), established on 13 February 1949. On 26 November 1981, the [Olympic Council of Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Council_of_Asia "Olympic Council of Asia") (OCA) replaced the AGF, and India became a member of the OCA.

India hosted the first [Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Games "Asian Games") in [1951](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1951_Asian_Games "1951 Asian Games") at [New Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Delhi "New Delhi"). The [2026 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Asian_Games "2026 Asian Games") were held in [Nagoya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagoya "Nagoya") and other parts of [Aichi Prefecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aichi_Prefecture "Aichi Prefecture") in Japan from 19 September to 4 October. The 2026 edition marked India's [20th consecutive appearance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_at_the_Asian_Games "India at the Asian Games") at the Asian Games. India's best performance came in the [2023 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Asian_Games "2023 Asian Games") at [Hangzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hangzhou "Hangzhou") where the country finished fourth in the [medal table](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Asian_Games_medal_table "2023 Asian Games medal table") with 106 medals including 28 gold, 38 silver, 40 bronze medals.

On 22 August 2026, the [Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Youth_Affairs_and_Sports "Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports") announced a 492-member Indian contingent for the Games. On 2 September 2026, the ministry released a revised list of 503 athletes and 265 support staff for the Games. The final traveling contingent consisted of 495 athletes. Sahdev Yadav, the treasurer of the IOA, was the [Chef de Mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chef_de_Mission "Chef de Mission") with [Sharath Kamal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharath_Kamal "Sharath Kamal") chosen as his deputy. On 18 September 2026, [Manu Bhaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manu_Bhaker "Manu Bhaker") and [Tajinderpal Singh Toor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tajinderpal_Singh_Toor "Tajinderpal Singh Toor") were announced as the nation's flag-bearers during the opening ceremony. However, Toor withdrew from the opening ceremony after the arrival of his kit was delayed and was replaced by [Pawan Sehrawat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pawan_Sehrawat "Pawan Sehrawat") as the flag bearer. Shooter [Neeru Dhanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neeru_Dhanda "Neeru Dhanda") was the flag-bearer for the closing ceremony.

### Broadcasting

[Sony Pictures Networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sony_Pictures_Networks "Sony Pictures Networks") acquired the broadcast rights for the country, with [Sony Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sony_Sports_Network "Sony Sports Network") as the television broadcaster and [SonyLIV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SonyLIV "SonyLIV") as the digital broadcaster.

## Medal summary

India finished fourth in the [medal table](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Asian_Games_medal_table "2026 Asian Games medal table") with 85 medals including 21 gold, 27 silver, and 37 bronze medals. It was their 2nd best finish ever after the [2022 edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_at_the_2022_Asian_Games "India at the 2022 Asian Games").

**Gold medalists**

The [Indian women's cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_women%27s_cricket_team "Indian women's cricket team") won India's first gold in this edition, defending their title in the [women's cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cricket_at_the_2026_Asian_Games_%E2%80%93_Women%27s_tournament "Cricket at the 2026 Asian Games – Women's tournament") event. [Suruchi Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suruchi_Singh "Suruchi Singh") and [Kamaljeet Sahlot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamaljeet_(sport_shooter) "Kamaljeet (sport shooter)") claimed the country's second gold medal, setting an impressive Asian record in the [mixed 10 metre air pistol shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooting_at_the_2026_Asian_Games_-_Mixed_10_metre_air_pistol_team "Shooting at the 2026 Asian Games - Mixed 10 metre air pistol team") event. This was the nation's first-ever gold medal in this event at the Asian Games. The [Indian men's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_national_kabaddi_team "India national kabaddi team") and [women's kabaddi teams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_women%27s_national_kabaddi_team "India women's national kabaddi team") won the country's next two gold medals, defending their titles successfully in both the [men's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi_at_the_2026_Asian_Games_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_tournament "Kabaddi at the 2026 Asian Games – Men's tournament") and [women's team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi_at_the_2026_Asian_Games_%E2%80%93_Women%27s_tournament "Kabaddi at the 2026 Asian Games – Women's tournament") events respectively. Neeru Dhanda won India's fifth gold medal and became the country's first-ever female trap shooting gold medalist in the [women's trap shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooting_at_the_2026_Asian_Games_%E2%80%93_Women%27s_trap "Shooting at the 2026 Asian Games – Women's trap") event and also set a new Asian record. This victory marked India's first trap gold in 48 years. [Kiran Pahal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiran_Pahal "Kiran Pahal"), [Prachi Choudhary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prachi_Choudhary "Prachi Choudhary"), [Poovamma Machettira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poovamma_Machettira "Poovamma Machettira"), and [Vithya Ramraj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vithya_Ramraj "Vithya Ramraj") won the country's sixth and first athletics gold medal in this edition in the [women's 4 × 400 metres relay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athletics_at_the_2026_Asian_Games_%E2%80%93_Women%27s_4_%C3%97_400_metres_relay "Athletics at the 2026 Asian Games – Women's 4 × 400 metres relay") event. [Chikitha Taniparthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chikitha_Taniparthi "Chikitha Taniparthi"), [Jyothi Surekha Vennam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyothi_Surekha_Vennam "Jyothi Surekha Vennam"), and [Prithika Pradeep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prithika_Pradeep "Prithika Pradeep") retained their title in the women's team compound event. Taniparthi and [Sahil Jadhav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahil_Jadhav "Sahil Jadhav") won the [compound mixed team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archery_at_the_2026_Asian_Games_%E2%80%93_Mixed_team_compound "Archery at the 2026 Asian Games – Mixed team compound") title, securing the first ever compound archery quota for the [2028 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2028_Summer_Olympics "2028 Summer Olympics").

## Archery

The [Archery Association of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archery_Association_of_India "Archery Association of India") held the selection trials for the Asian Games at the [Sports Authority of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_Authority_of_India "Sports Authority of India") (SAI) facility in [Sonipat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonipat "Sonipat") in May 2026. A total of 12 athletes were selected across the [recurve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recurve_bow "Recurve bow") and [compound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compound_bow "Compound bow") events.

Key

* **Q** = Qualified for the next round
* **W** = Win
* **L** = Loss

## Athletics

India initially announced a 79-member squad to compete in the athletic events. Runner Abhishek Pal failed a dope test, hurdler Tejas Shirse pulled out due to an injury, and [Annu Rani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annu_Rani "Annu Rani"), Nithya Gandhe, and [Shahnavaz Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahnavaz_Khan "Shahnavaz Khan") were dropped from the squad. [Javelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javelin "Javelin") thrower [Yashvir Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yashvir_Singh_(javelin_thrower) "Yashvir Singh (javelin thrower)") replaced the injured [Neeraj Chopra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neeraj_Chopra "Neeraj Chopra"), while long jumper David Solomon replaced Khan. The final squad consisted of 75 athletes competing in 41 events. On 26 September 2026, [Jyothi Yarraji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jyothi_Yarraji "Jyothi Yarraji") did not compete in the women's 100 m hurdles heats, and [Praveen Chithravel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Praveen_Chithravel "Praveen Chithravel") pulled out after his first jump in the men's triple jump due to an injury.

## Boxing

The [Boxing Federation of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxing_Federation_of_India "Boxing Federation of India") organised national trials in May 2026, and announced a 11-member squad for the Games. The men's chief coach is [C. A. Kuttappa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C._A._Kuttappa "C. A. Kuttappa").

## Cricket

Both the [men's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_men%27s_national_cricket_team "India men's national cricket team") and [women's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_women%27s_national_cricket_team "India women's national cricket team") cricket teams qualified for the Games as [full members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_International_Cricket_Council_members "List of International Cricket Council members") of the [International Cricket Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Cricket_Council "International Cricket Council"). The [Board of Control for Cricket in India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Board_of_Control_for_Cricket_in_India "Board of Control for Cricket in India") announced the 15-member men's squad on 6 June 2026, which was captained by [Shreyas Iyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shreyas_Iyer "Shreyas Iyer") and coached by [VVS Laxman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VVS_Laxman "VVS Laxman"). The women's squad, led by [Harmanpreet Kaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harmanpreet_Kaur "Harmanpreet Kaur"), was announced on 30 June. Subsequently, [Harshit Rana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harshit_Rana "Harshit Rana") and [Varun Chakravarthy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varun_Chakravarthy "Varun Chakravarthy") were replaced by [Yash Thakur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yash_Thakur "Yash Thakur") and [Vipraj Nigam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vipraj_Nigam "Vipraj Nigam") respectively due to injuries and [Nitish Kumar Reddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nitish_Kumar_Reddy "Nitish Kumar Reddy") was withdrawn from the men's squad. In the women's squad, [Jemimah Rodrigues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jemimah_Rodrigues "Jemimah Rodrigues") was ruled out due to an injury and replaced by [Pratika Rawal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pratika_Rawal "Pratika Rawal"). The men's team received a bye in the preliminary stage and directly qualified for the knockout stage.

## Equestrian

The [Equestrian Federation of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equestrian_Federation_of_India "Equestrian Federation of India") selected a 12-member squad for the three disciplines at the Games in June 2026. The show jumping team was omitted from the final list, which reduced the squad strength to eight. Aahan Kumar, who was supposed to compete in the eventing competitions, was unable to participate after his horse, *Bolivar Gio Granno*, tested positive for [Piroplasmosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piroplasmosis "Piroplasmosis") a few weeks before the Games.

* **L** = Loss
* **Q** = Qualified for the next round

## Field hockey

The Indian [men's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_men%27s_national_field_hockey_team "India men's national field hockey team") and [women's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_women%27s_national_field_hockey_team "India women's national field hockey team") teams qualified for the Games by finishing in the top six in the [previous edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_hockey_at_the_2022_Asian_Games "Field hockey at the 2022 Asian Games"), with the men's team entering the tournament as the [defending champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_hockey_at_the_2022_Asian_Games_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_tournament "Field hockey at the 2022 Asian Games – Men's tournament"). [Hockey India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hockey_India "Hockey India") announced the 20-member women's team for the Games on 10 July 2026, and 20-member men's squad on 30 July.

## Judo

The judo selection trials for the Games were held in February 2026 at the [K. D. Jadhav Indoor Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K._D._Jadhav_Indoor_Hall "K. D. Jadhav Indoor Hall") in Delhi, with 12 athletes qualifying. However, the sports ministry later excluded several athletes (nine) from the initial list including [2026 Commonwealth Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Commonwealth_Games "2026 Commonwealth Games") medalists [Harsh Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harsh_Singh_(judoka) "Harsh Singh (judoka)") and [Unnati Sharma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unnati_Sharma "Unnati Sharma"), and approved a final list of three judokas for the Games.

| Opposition Result | Opposition Result | Opposition Result | Opposition Result | Opposition Result |

* **W** = Win
* **L** = Loss

* s2 = *[Shido](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judo#Penalties "Judo")* 2
* s3 = *[Hansoku-make](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hansoku-make "Hansoku-make")* resulting from 'progressive' penalties awarded during the contest

* **L** = Loss
* **Q** = Qualified for the next round

## Shooting

In May 2026, the [National Rifle Association of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rifle_Association_of_India "National Rifle Association of India") announced a 12-member squad for the [shotgun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shotgun "Shotgun") events at the Games. In June 2026, the squads for the [pistol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pistol "Pistol") and [rifle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rifle "Rifle") events were announced, which included 18 shooters. Aakash Bharadwaj replaced Gaurav Mansoori in the squad after the latter failed a doping test.

## Squash

The [Squash Rackets Federation of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Squash_Rackets_Federation_of_India "Squash Rackets Federation of India") announced an eight-member squad for the Games in June 2026. [Joshna Chinappa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joshna_Chinappa "Joshna Chinappa") qualified for her seventh consecutive Asian Games, the most by any Indian.

## Surfing

## Tennis

A 12-member tennis squad for the Games was announced by the [All India Tennis Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Tennis_Association "All India Tennis Association") in June 2026. ## Weightlifting

## Wrestling

The [Wrestling Federation of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wrestling_Federation_of_India "Wrestling Federation of India") held selection trials for the [freestyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freestyle_wrestling "Freestyle wrestling") and [Greco-Roman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greco-Roman_wrestling "Greco-Roman wrestling") events on 30 and 31 May 2026 respectively. An 18-member squad for the Games was announced at the conclusion of the trials. Subsequently, Mukul Dahiya, Deepanshu Ahlawat, and Deepak were removed from the squad after failing a doping test, thus dropping the squad strength to 15.

**Key**:

* **W** – Win
* **L** – Loss
* VFA – [Victory by Fall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pin_(amateur_wrestling) "Pin (amateur wrestling)")
* VFO – Victory by Forfeit
* VPO – Victory by Points – without any point scored by opponent
* VPO1 – Victory by Points – with point(s) scored by opponent
* VSU – Victory by technical superiority – without any point scored by the opponent
* VSU1 – Victory by technical superiority – with point(s) scored by the opponent
